======================================================================
[D] Transient radiance constant: ICNIRP 2.0e4 vs IEC 5.0e4
======================================================================
  [INF] D1  ICNIRP 2013 basic retinal-thermal radiance EL constant is 2.0*10^4 (Eq 6, valid 1 us..0.25 s); the pipeline implements the IEC 62471/BS EN version 5e4/(alpha*t^0.25) (Eq 4.9) which is documented in validate_norm_vs_code [E]. Difference: at the same sub-second t the ICNIRP EL would be ~2.5x lower (more exclusive).
  [OK ] D2  ratio check at t=0.001 s: 5e4/2e4 = 2.5  -- 2.81e+05 / 1.12e+05
  [OK ] D2  ratio check at t=0.01 s: 5e4/2e4 = 2.5  -- 1.58e+05 / 6.32e+04
  [OK ] D2  ratio check at t=0.1 s: 5e4/2e4 = 2.5  -- 8.89e+04 / 3.56e+04
  [OK ] D2  ratio check at t=0.25 s: 5e4/2e4 = 2.5  -- 7.07e+04 / 2.83e+04
  [INF] D3  The two formulations agree at the steady boundary: 2.0e4*0.25^-0.25 = 2.83e4/alpha ~= 2.8e4/alpha (Table 4 t>=0.25s) and 5e4*10^-0.25 = 2.81e4/alpha (code t=10 s evaluation). RG classification is unchanged because every current-config hit sits far above the steady ELV either way (see section [I]).

[G] Weak visual stimulus (Eqs 8/9 + errata)
======================================================================
  [OK ] G1  Eq 8 WVS radiance 2.0e4*alpha^-1*t^-0.25  -- needle 'L_WVS^EL=2.0*10^4*α^-1*t^-0.25W/(m^2*sr)(for0.25s<t<100s)' found
  [OK ] G2  Eq 9 corrected 6300/alpha (t >= 100 s)  -- needle 'L_WVS^EL=0.63*α^-1*10^4=6300/αW/(m^2*sr)' found
  [INF] G3  ICNIRP uses 6300/alpha for weak-visual-stimulus t>=100 s; the IEC/BS EN Table 6.1 weak-stimulus L_IR EL is 6000/alpha (5%% difference). The pipeline does NOT implement a separate weak-stimulus clause (IEC position, validated in validate_norm_vs_code I2); on a solar glare beam the strong visual stimulus is unambiguous, so the 6000 vs 6300 choice is irrelevant here.

  [OK ] H2  ICNIRP R(440) = 1 (cap 1.0, no hump)  -- got 1
  [OK ] H2  ICNIRP R(500) = 1 (cap 1.0, no hump)  -- got 1

  [OK ] H4  peak diverge: IEC/IEC- vs ICNIRP-style R at 435 = 10.0 vs 1.0  -- IEC 10.000 vs ICNIRP 1.000
  [OK ] H5  k_R under ICNIRP R() in (0.3, 0.8) and < IEC k_R 1.267  -- ICNIRP 0.5838 vs IEC 1.2675 (ratio 2.17)
  [INF] H6  k_R = 0.5838 under ICNIRP 2013 Table 2 R() vs k_R = 1.2675 under the CIE/IEC Table 4.2 R() used by the pipeline. The difference is the blue-violet hump (peak 10.0 at 435-440 nm) that IEC 62471 adopts for the burn function but ICNIRP 2013 does not. Both are legitimate references; the pipeline deliberately follows IEC 62471 (the product-safety standard the report cites). Section [I] shows the RG verdict survives the stricter-independent ICNIRP ELV check.
  [INF] H7  B(lambda) is shared by both documents (knot 385: ICNIRP 0.0125 vs IEC Table 4.2 0.013, <2%%); the blue-light clause is therefore not affected by the R(lambda) divergence.

======================================================================
[J] INFO: documented simplifications / conventions
======================================================================
  [INF] J1  alpha_min divergence ICNIRP 0.0015 rad vs IEC 0.0017 rad is benign on the current config: every hazard alpha sits at ~0.05 rad (sun-disc-limited far field) or ~0.05 rad (foreshortened vehicles), far above both flooring values, so the clamp never engages.
  [INF] J2  200 mm closest-viewing distance (Sec 11) is not applied: all receptors are >= 10 m from the panel (site geometry), and the default IEC/SGHAT observer is a point at its georeferenced position, not at the 0.2 m near-point.
  [INF] J3  ICNIRP integration ranges are printed inconsistently: in-body Eqs 4/10/11 print 380-1400 nm for both L_R and L_B; the errata page 87 line corrects Eq 10/11 to 300-700 nm for the blue-light hazard (B(λ)=0.01 below 380 makes the difference negligible); Eq 4 (thermal) legitimately stays 380-1400 nm because ICNIRP's R(lambda) tails cover 1400. The pipeline integrates the full 200-4000 nm spectrum once for k_X, so it is insensitive to this editorial inconsistency.
  [INF] J4  ICNIRP is an ELV guideline (limits only), NOT a risk-group taxonomy; the RG1/RG2/RG3 classification is IEC 62471 Table 6.1's. The mapping used here -- RG3/IEC == exceeds the ICNIRP steady retina ELV (2.8e4/alpha) -- is our documented bridge between the two references and is exactly what section [I] asserts.
  [INF] J5  dose vs average-power: ICNIRP permits checking either the radiance dose D_R or the average radiance L_R; the pipeline uses the radiance form (L_R) with the t_max inversion, which is the more readable safety statement for a continuous glare minute (Eq 4.9/6 inverse). The dose cross-check is redundant given the constant-radiance assumption.
  [INF] J6  89-degree grazing clip, DNI floor and the sun-disc front-face guard are SGHAT/pipeline additions, orthogonal to the standard limits and already documented -- this script only validates the exposure-limit reference chain.

Summary: 79 passed, 4 failed, 16 info

======================================================================
[J] INFO: documented simplifications / conventions
======================================================================
  [INF] J1  alpha_min divergence ICNIRP 0.0015 rad vs IEC 0.0017 rad is benign on the current config: every hazard alpha sits at ~0.05 rad (sun-disc-limited far field) or ~0.05 rad (foreshortened vehicles), far above both flooring values, so the clamp never engages.
  [INF] J2  200 mm closest-viewing distance (Sec 11) is not applied: all receptors are >= 10 m from the panel (site geometry), and the default IEC/SGHAT observer is a point at its georeferenced position, not at the 0.2 m near-point.
  [INF] J3  ICNIRP integration ranges are printed inconsistently: in-body Eqs 4/10/11 print 380-1400 nm for both L_R and L_B; the errata page 87 line corrects Eq 10/11 to 300-700 nm for the blue-light hazard (B(λ)=0.01 below 380 makes the difference negligible); Eq 4 (thermal) legitimately stays 380-1400 nm because ICNIRP's R(lambda) tails cover 1400. The pipeline integrates the full 200-4000 nm spectrum once for k_X, so it is insensitive to this editorial inconsistency.
  [INF] J4  ICNIRP is an ELV guideline (limits only), NOT a risk-group taxonomy; the RG1/RG2/RG3 classification is IEC 62471 Table 6.1's. The mapping used here -- RG3/IEC == exceeds the ICNIRP steady retina ELV (2.8e4/alpha) -- is our documented bridge between the two references and is exactly what section [I] asserts.
  [INF] J5  dose vs average-power: ICNIRP permits checking either the radiance dose D_R or the average radiance L_R; the pipeline uses the radiance form (L_R) with the t_max inversion, which is the more readable safety statement for a continuous glare minute (Eq 4.9/6 inverse). The dose cross-check is redundant given the constant-radiance assumption.
  [INF] J6  89-degree grazing clip, DNI floor and the sun-disc front-face guard are SGHAT/pipeline additions, orthogonal to the standard limits and already documented -- this script only validates the exposure-limit reference chain.

Summary: 83 passed, 1 failed, 17 info

  [INF] I3  [az278 vehicle_3] safe-stare: t_max_ICNIRP=0.0104 s vs t_max_IEC=0.0183 s (both < 0.25 s -> transient regime, ICNIRP more exclusive by ~2.5^4 = 1.8x)
  [OK ] I5  evaluated >= 1 receptor pair  -- 4

======================================================================
[J] INFO: documented simplifications / conventions
======================================================================
  [INF] J1  alpha_min divergence ICNIRP 0.0015 rad vs IEC 0.0017 rad is benign on the current config: every hazard alpha sits at ~0.05 rad (sun-disc-limited far field) or ~0.05 rad (foreshortened vehicles), far above both flooring values, so the clamp never engages.
  [INF] J2  200 mm closest-viewing distance (Sec 11) is not applied: all receptors are >= 10 m from the panel (site geometry), and the default IEC/SGHAT observer is a point at its georeferenced position, not at the 0.2 m near-point.
  [INF] J3  ICNIRP integration ranges are printed inconsistently: in-body Eqs 4/10/11 print 380-1400 nm for both L_R and L_B; the errata page 87 line corrects Eq 10/11 to 300-700 nm for the blue-light hazard (B(λ)=0.01 below 380 makes the difference negligible); Eq 4 (thermal) legitimately stays 380-1400 nm because ICNIRP's R(lambda) tails cover 1400. The pipeline integrates the full 200-4000 nm spectrum once for k_X, so it is insensitive to this editorial inconsistency.
  [INF] J4  ICNIRP is an ELV guideline (limits only), NOT a risk-group taxonomy; the RG1/RG2/RG3 classification is IEC 62471 Table 6.1's. The mapping used here -- RG3/IEC == exceeds the ICNIRP steady retina ELV (2.8e4/alpha) -- is our documented bridge between the two references and is exactly what section [I] asserts.
  [INF] J5  dose vs average-power: ICNIRP permits checking either the radiance dose D_R or the average radiance L_R; the pipeline uses the radiance form (L_R) with the t_max inversion, which is the more readable safety statement for a continuous glare minute (Eq 4.9/6 inverse). The dose cross-check is redundant given the constant-radiance assumption.
  [INF] J6  89-degree grazing clip, DNI floor and the sun-disc front-face guard are SGHAT/pipeline additions, orthogonal to the standard limits and already documented -- this script only validates the exposure-limit reference chain.

Summary: 83 passed, 1 failed, 17 info

RG classification is unchanged because every current-config hit sits far above the steady ELV either way (see section [I]).

======================================================================
[J] INFO: documented simplifications / conventions
======================================================================
  [INF] J1  alpha_min divergence ICNIRP 0.0015 rad vs IEC 0.0017 rad is benign on the current config: every hazard alpha sits at ~0.05 rad (sun-disc-limited far field) or ~0.05 rad (foreshortened vehicles), far above both flooring values, so the clamp never engages.
  [INF] J2  200 mm closest-viewing distance (Sec 11) is not applied: all receptors are >= 10 m from the panel (site geometry), and the default IEC/SGHAT observer is a point at its georeferenced position, not at the 0.2 m near-point.
  [INF] J3  ICNIRP integration ranges are printed inconsistently: in-body Eqs 4/10/11 print 380-1400 nm for both L_R and L_B; the errata page 87 line corrects Eq 10/11 to 300-700 nm for the blue-light hazard (B(λ)=0.01 below 380 makes the difference negligible); Eq 4 (thermal) legitimately stays 380-1400 nm because ICNIRP's R(lambda) tails cover 1400. The pipeline integrates the full 200-4000 nm spectrum once for k_X, so it is insensitive to this editorial inconsistency.
  [INF] J4  ICNIRP is an ELV guideline (limits only), NOT a risk-group taxonomy; the RG1/RG2/RG3 classification is IEC 62471 Table 6.1's. The mapping used here -- RG3/IEC == exceeds the ICNIRP steady retina ELV (2.8e4/alpha) -- is our documented bridge between the two references and is exactly what section [I] asserts.
  [INF] J5  dose vs average-power: ICNIRP permits checking either the radiance dose D_R or the average radiance L_R; the pipeline uses the radiance form (L_R) with the t_max inversion, which is the more readable safety statement for a continuous glare minute (Eq 4.9/6 inverse). The dose cross-check is redundant given the constant-radiance assumption.
  [INF] J6  89-degree grazing clip, DNI floor and the sun-disc front-face guard are SGHAT/pipeline additions, orthogonal to the standard limits and already documented -- this script only validates the exposure-limit reference chain.

Summary: 84 passed, 0 failed, 17 info

  [INF] H6  k_R = 0.5838 under ICNIRP 2013 Table 2 R() vs k_R = 1.2675 under the CIE/IEC Table 4.2 R() used by the pipeline. The difference is the blue-violet hump (peak 10.0 at 435-440 nm) that IEC 62471 adopts for the burn function but ICNIRP 2013 does not. Both are legitimate references; the pipeline deliberately follows IEC 62471 (the product-safety standard the report cites). Section [I] shows the RG verdict survives the stricter-independent ICNIRP ELV check.
  [INF] H7  B(lambda) is shared by both documents (knot 385: ICNIRP 0.0125 vs IEC Table 4.2 0.013, <2%%); the blue-light clause is therefore not affected by the R(lambda) divergence.

[J] INFO: documented simplifications / conventions
======================================================================
  [INF] J1  alpha_min divergence ICNIRP 0.0015 rad vs IEC 0.0017 rad is benign on the current config: every hazard alpha sits at ~0.05 rad (sun-disc-limited far field) or ~0.05 rad (foreshortened vehicles), far above both flooring values, so the clamp never engages.
  [INF] J2  200 mm closest-viewing distance (Sec 11) is not applied: all receptors are >= 10 m from the panel (site geometry), and the default IEC/SGHAT observer is a point at its georeferenced position, not at the 0.2 m near-point.
  [INF] J3  ICNIRP integration ranges are printed inconsistently: in-body Eqs 4/10/11 print 380-1400 nm for both L_R and L_B; the errata page 87 line corrects Eq 10/11 to 300-700 nm for the blue-light hazard (B(λ)=0.01 below 380 makes the difference negligible); Eq 4 (thermal) legitimately stays 380-1400 nm because ICNIRP's R(lambda) tails cover 1400. The pipeline integrates the full 200-4000 nm spectrum once for k_X, so it is insensitive to this editorial inconsistency.
  [INF] J4  ICNIRP is an ELV guideline (limits only), NOT a risk-group taxonomy; the RG1/RG2/RG3 classification is IEC 62471 Table 6.1's. The mapping used here -- RG3/IEC == exceeds the ICNIRP steady retina ELV (2.8e4/alpha) -- is our documented bridge between the two references and is exactly what section [I] asserts.
  [INF] J5  dose vs average-power: ICNIRP permits checking either the radiance dose D_R or the average radiance L_R; the pipeline uses the radiance form (L_R) with the t_max inversion, which is the more readable safety statement for a continuous glare minute (Eq 4.9/6 inverse). The dose cross-check is redundant given the constant-radiance assumption.
  [INF] J6  89-degree grazing clip, DNI floor and the sun-disc front-face guard are SGHAT/pipeline additions, orthogonal to the standard limits and already documented -- this script only validates the exposure-limit reference chain.

Summary: 84 passed, 0 failed, 17 info

  [OK ] D2  ratio check at t=0.25 s: 5e4/2e4 = 2.5  -- 7.07e+04 / 2.83e+04
  [INF] D3  The two formulations agree at the steady boundary: 2.0e4*0.25^-0.25 = 2.83e4/alpha ~= 2.8e4/alpha (Table 4 t>=0.25s) and 5e4*10^-0.25 = 2.81e4/alpha (code t=10 s evaluation). RG classification is unchanged because every current-config hit sits far above the steady ELV either way (see section [I]).

======================================================================
[J] INFO: documented simplifications / conventions
======================================================================
  [INF] J1  alpha_min divergence ICNIRP 0.0015 rad vs IEC 0.0017 rad is benign on the current config: every hazard alpha sits at ~0.05 rad (sun-disc-limited far field) or ~0.05 rad (foreshortened vehicles), far above both flooring values, so the clamp never engages.
  [INF] J2  200 mm closest-viewing distance (Sec 11) is not applied: all receptors are >= 10 m from the panel (site geometry), and the default IEC/SGHAT observer is a point at its georeferenced position, not at the 0.2 m near-point.
  [INF] J3  ICNIRP integration ranges are printed inconsistently: in-body Eqs 4/10/11 print 380-1400 nm for both L_R and L_B; the errata page 87 line corrects Eq 10/11 to 300-700 nm for the blue-light hazard (B(λ)=0.01 below 380 makes the difference negligible); Eq 4 (thermal) legitimately stays 380-1400 nm because ICNIRP's R(lambda) tails cover 1400. The pipeline integrates the full 200-4000 nm spectrum once for k_X, so it is insensitive to this editorial inconsistency.
  [INF] J4  ICNIRP is an ELV guideline (limits only), NOT a risk-group taxonomy; the RG1/RG2/RG3 classification is IEC 62471 Table 6.1's. The mapping used here -- RG3/IEC == exceeds the ICNIRP steady retina ELV (2.8e4/alpha) -- is our documented bridge between the two references and is exactly what section [I] asserts.
  [INF] J5  dose vs average-power: ICNIRP permits checking either the radiance dose D_R or the average radiance L_R; the pipeline uses the radiance form (L_R) with the t_max inversion, which is the more readable safety statement for a continuous glare minute (Eq 4.9/6 inverse). The dose cross-check is redundant given the constant-radiance assumption.
  [INF] J6  89-degree grazing clip, DNI floor and the sun-disc front-face guard are SGHAT/pipeline additions, orthogonal to the standard limits and already documented -- this script only validates the exposure-limit reference chain.

Summary: 84 passed, 0 failed, 17 info

======================================================================
[J] INFO: documented simplifications / conventions
======================================================================
  [INF] J1  alpha_min divergence ICNIRP 0.0015 rad vs IEC 0.0017 rad is benign on the current config: every hazard alpha sits at ~0.05 rad (sun-disc-limited far field) or ~0.05 rad (foreshortened vehicles), far above both flooring values, so the clamp never engages.
  [INF] J2  200 mm closest-viewing distance (Sec 11) is not applied: all receptors are >= 10 m from the panel (site geometry), and the default IEC/SGHAT observer is a point at its georeferenced position, not at the 0.2 m near-point.
  [INF] J3  ICNIRP integration ranges are printed inconsistently: in-body Eqs 4/10/11 print 380-1400 nm for both L_R and L_B; the errata page 87 line corrects Eq 10/11 to 300-700 nm for the blue-light hazard (B(λ)=0.01 below 380 makes the difference negligible); Eq 4 (thermal) legitimately stays 380-1400 nm because ICNIRP's R(lambda) tails cover 1400. The pipeline integrates the full 200-4000 nm spectrum once for k_X, so it is insensitive to this editorial inconsistency.
  [INF] J4  ICNIRP is an ELV guideline (limits only), NOT a risk-group taxonomy; the RG1/RG2/RG3 classification is IEC 62471 Table 6.1's. The mapping used here -- RG3/IEC == exceeds the ICNIRP steady retina ELV (2.8e4/alpha) -- is our documented bridge between the two references and is exactly what section [I] asserts.
  [INF] J5  dose vs average-power: ICNIRP permits checking either the radiance dose D_R or the average radiance L_R; the pipeline uses the radiance form (L_R) with the t_max inversion, which is the more readable safety statement for a continuous glare minute (Eq 4.9/6 inverse). The dose cross-check is redundant given the constant-radiance assumption.
  [INF] J6  89-degree grazing clip, DNI floor and the sun-disc front-face guard are SGHAT/pipeline additions, orthogonal to the standard limits and already documented -- this script only validates the exposure-limit reference chain.

Summary: 90 passed, 0 failed, 20 info

  [OK ] I2  [az278 west1_route_2] IEC k_R * L also exceeds same ELV (redundant)  -- 4.31x
  [INF] I3  [az278 west1_route_2] safe-stare: t_max_ICNIRP=0.0168 s vs t_max_IEC=0.0296 s (both < 0.25 s -> transient regime, ICNIRP more exclusive by ~2.5^4 = 1.8x)
  [OK ] I5  evaluated >= 1 receptor pair  -- 4

======================================================================
[J] INFO: documented simplifications / conventions
======================================================================
  [INF] J1  alpha_min divergence ICNIRP 0.0015 rad vs IEC 0.0017 rad is benign on the current config: every hazard alpha sits at ~0.05 rad (sun-disc-limited far field), far above both flooring values, so the clamp never engages.
  [INF] J2  200 mm closest-viewing distance (Sec 11) is not applied: all receptors are >= 10 m from the panel (site geometry), and the default IEC/SGHAT observer is a point at its georeferenced position, not at the 0.2 m near-point.
  [INF] J3  ICNIRP integration ranges are printed inconsistently: in-body Eqs 4/10/11 print 380-1400 nm for both L_R and L_B; the errata page 87 line corrects Eq 10/11 to 300-700 nm for the blue-light hazard (B(λ)=0.01 below 380 makes the difference negligible); Eq 4 (thermal) legitimately stays 380-1400 nm because ICNIRP's R(lambda) tails cover 1400. The pipeline integrates the full 200-4000 nm spectrum once for k_X, so it is insensitive to this editorial inconsistency.
  [INF] J4  ICNIRP is an ELV guideline (limits only), NOT a risk-group taxonomy; the RG1/RG2/RG3 classification is IEC 62471 Table 6.1's. The mapping used here -- RG3/IEC == exceeds the ICNIRP steady retina ELV (2.8e4/alpha) -- is our documented bridge between the two references and is exactly what section [I] asserts.
  [INF] J5  dose vs average-power: ICNIRP permits checking either the radiance dose D_R or the average radiance L_R; the pipeline uses the radiance form (L_R) with the t_max inversion, which is the more readable safety statement for a continuous glare minute (Eq 4.9/6 inverse). The dose cross-check is redundant given the constant-radiance assumption.
  [INF] J6  89-degree grazing clip, DNI floor and the sun-disc front-face guard are SGHAT/pipeline additions, orthogonal to the standard limits and already documented -- this script only validates the exposure-limit reference chain.

Summary: 84 passed, 0 failed, 17 info

  [INF] D1  ICNIRP 2013 basic retinal-thermal radiance EL constant is 2.0*10^4 (Eq 6, valid 1 us..0.25 s); the pipeline implements the IEC 62471/BS EN version 5e4/(alpha*t^0.25) (Eq 4.9) which is documented in validate_norm_vs_code [E].   [INF] D3  The two formulations agree at the steady boundary: 2.0e4*0.25^-0.25 = 2.83e4/alpha ~= 2.8e4/alpha (Table 4 t>=0.25s) and 5e4*10^-0.25 = 2.81e4/alpha (code t=10 s evaluation). RG classification is unchanged because every current-config hit sits far above the steady ELV either way (see section [I]).

[D] Transient radiance constant: ICNIRP 2.0e4 vs IEC 5.0e4
======================================================================
  [INF] D1  ICNIRP 2013 basic retinal-thermal radiance EL constant is 2.0*10^4 (Eq 6, valid 1 us..0.25 s); the pipeline implements the IEC 62471/BS EN version 5e4/(alpha*t^0.25) (Eq 4.9) which is documented in validate_norm_vs_code [E]. Difference: at the same sub-second t the ICNIRP EL would be ~2.5x lower (more exclusive).
  [OK ] D2  ratio check at t=0.001 s: 5e4/2e4 = 2.5  -- 2.81e+05 / 1.12e+05

  [OK ] D2  ratio check at t=0.25 s: 5e4/2e4 = 2.5  -- 7.07e+04 / 2.83e+04
  [INF] D3  The two formulations agree at the steady boundary: 2.0e4*0.25^-0.25 = 2.83e4/alpha ~= 2.8e4/alpha (Table 4 t>=0.25s) and 5e4*10^-0.25 = 2.81e4/alpha (code t=10 s evaluation). RG classification is unchanged because every current-config hit sits far above the steady ELV either way (see section [I]).

======================================================================
[J] INFO: documented simplifications / conventions
======================================================================
  [INF] J1  alpha_min divergence ICNIRP 0.0015 rad vs IEC 0.0017 rad is benign on the current config: every hazard alpha sits at ~0.05 rad (sun-disc-limited far field), far above both flooring values, so the clamp never engages.
  [INF] J2  200 mm closest-viewing distance (Sec 11) is not applied: all receptors are >= 10 m from the panel (site geometry), and the default IEC/SGHAT observer is a point at its georeferenced position, not at the 0.2 m near-point.
  [INF] J3  ICNIRP integration ranges are printed inconsistently: in-body Eqs 4/10/11 print 380-1400 nm for both L_R and L_B; the errata page 87 line corrects Eq 10/11 to 300-700 nm for the blue-light hazard (B(λ)=0.01 below 380 makes the difference negligible); Eq 4 (thermal) legitimately stays 380-1400 nm because ICNIRP's R(lambda) tails cover 1400. The pipeline integrates the full 200-4000 nm spectrum once for k_X, so it is insensitive to this editorial inconsistency.
  [INF] J4  ICNIRP is an ELV guideline (limits only), NOT a risk-group taxonomy; the RG1/RG2/RG3 classification is IEC 62471 Table 6.1's. The mapping used here -- RG3/IEC == exceeds the ICNIRP steady retina ELV (2.8e4/alpha) -- is our documented bridge between the two references and is exactly what section [I] asserts.
  [INF] J5  dose vs average-power: ICNIRP permits checking either the radiance dose D_R or the average radiance L_R; the pipeline uses the radiance form (L_R) with the t_max inversion, which is the more readable safety statement for a continuous glare minute (Eq 4.9/6 inverse). The dose cross-check is redundant given the constant-radiance assumption.
  [INF] J6  89-degree grazing clip, DNI floor and the sun-disc front-face guard are SGHAT/pipeline additions, orthogonal to the standard limits and already documented -- this script only validates the exposure-limit reference chain.

Summary: 84 passed, 0 failed, 17 info

[J] INFO: documented simplifications / conventions
======================================================================
  [INF] J1  alpha_min divergence ICNIRP 0.0015 rad vs IEC 0.0017 rad is benign on the current config: every hazard alpha sits at ~0.05 rad (sun-disc-limited far field), far above both flooring values, so the clamp never engages.
  [INF] J2  200 mm closest-viewing distance (Sec 11) is not applied: all receptors are >= 10 m from the panel (site geometry), and the default IEC/SGHAT observer is a point at its georeferenced position, not at the 0.2 m near-point.
  [INF] J3  ICNIRP integration ranges are printed inconsistently: in-body Eqs 4/10/11 print 380-1400 nm for both L_R and L_B; the errata page 87 line corrects Eq 10/11 to 300-700 nm for the blue-light hazard (B(λ)=0.01 below 380 makes the difference negligible); Eq 4 (thermal) legitimately stays 380-1400 nm because ICNIRP's R(lambda) tails cover 1400. The pipeline integrates the full 200-4000 nm spectrum once for k_X, so it is insensitive to this editorial inconsistency.
  [INF] J4  ICNIRP is an ELV guideline (limits only), NOT a risk-group taxonomy; the RG1/RG2/RG3 classification is IEC 62471 Table 6.1's. The mapping used here -- RG3/IEC == exceeds the ICNIRP steady retina ELV (2.8e4/alpha) -- is our documented bridge between the two references and is exactly what section [I] asserts.
  [INF] J5  dose vs average-power: ICNIRP permits checking either the radiance dose D_R or the average radiance L_R; the pipeline uses the radiance form (L_R) with the t_max inversion, which is the more readable safety statement for a continuous glare minute (Eq 4.9/6 inverse). The dose cross-check is redundant given the constant-radiance assumption.
  [INF] J6  89-degree grazing clip, DNI floor and the sun-disc front-face guard are SGHAT/pipeline additions, orthogonal to the standard limits and already documented -- this script only validates the exposure-limit reference chain.

Summary: 84 passed, 0 failed, 17 info

[D] Transient radiance constant: ICNIRP 2.0e4 vs IEC 5.0e4
======================================================================
  [INF] D1  ICNIRP 2013 basic retinal-thermal radiance EL constant is 2.0*10^4 (Eq 6, valid 1 us..0.25 s); the pipeline implements the IEC 62471/BS EN version 5e4/(alpha*t^0.25) (Eq 4.9) which is documented in validate_norm_vs_code [E]. Difference: at the same sub-second t the ICNIRP EL would be ~2.5x lower (more exclusive).
  [OK ] D2  ratio check at t=0.001 s: 5e4/2e4 = 2.5  -- 2.81e+05 / 1.12e+05
  [OK ] D2  ratio check at t=0.01 s: 5e4/2e4 = 2.5  -- 1.58e+05 / 6.32e+04

  [OK ] D2  ratio check at t=0.25 s: 5e4/2e4 = 2.5  -- 7.07e+04 / 2.83e+04
  [INF] D3  The two formulations agree at the steady boundary: 2.0e4*0.25^-0.25 = 2.83e4/alpha ~= 2.8e4/alpha (Table 4 t>=0.25s) and 5e4*10^-0.25 = 2.81e4/alpha (code t=10 s evaluation). On the committed 2-case output the az098 hits far exceed it and the az278 far-field hits now fall below it (see section [I]).

======================================================================
[J] INFO: documented simplifications / conventions
======================================================================
  [INF] J1  alpha_min divergence ICNIRP 0.0015 rad vs IEC 0.0017 rad is benign on the current config: every hazard alpha sits at ~0.05 rad (sun-disc-limited far field), far above both flooring values, so the clamp never engages.
  [INF] J2  200 mm closest-viewing distance (Sec 11) is not applied: all receptors are >= 10 m from the panel (site geometry), and the default IEC/SGHAT observer is a point at its georeferenced position, not at the 0.2 m near-point.
  [INF] J3  ICNIRP integration ranges are printed inconsistently: in-body Eqs 4/10/11 print 380-1400 nm for both L_R and L_B; the errata page 87 line corrects Eq 10/11 to 300-700 nm for the blue-light hazard (B(λ)=0.01 below 380 makes the difference negligible); Eq 4 (thermal) legitimately stays 380-1400 nm because ICNIRP's R(lambda) tails cover 1400. The pipeline integrates the full 200-4000 nm spectrum once for k_X, so it is insensitive to this editorial inconsistency.
  [INF] J4  ICNIRP is an ELV guideline (limits only), NOT a risk-group taxonomy; the RG1/RG2/RG3 classification is IEC 62471 Table 6.1's. The mapping used here -- RG3/IEC == exceeds the ICNIRP steady retina ELV (2.8e4/alpha) -- is our documented bridge between the two references and is exactly what section [I] asserts.
  [INF] J5  dose vs average-power: ICNIRP permits checking either the radiance dose D_R or the average radiance L_R; the pipeline uses the radiance form (L_R) with the t_max inversion, which is the more readable safety statement for a continuous glare minute (Eq 4.9/6 inverse). The dose cross-check is redundant given the constant-radiance assumption.
  [INF] J6  89-degree grazing clip, DNI floor and the sun-disc front-face guard are SGHAT/pipeline additions, orthogonal to the standard limits and already documented -- this script only validates the exposure-limit reference chain.

Summary: 84 passed, 0 failed, 17 info

======================================================================
[J] INFO: documented simplifications / conventions
======================================================================
  [INF] J1  alpha_min divergence ICNIRP 0.0015 rad vs IEC 0.0017 rad is benign on the current config: every hazard alpha sits at ~0.05 rad (sun-disc-limited far field), far above both flooring values, so the clamp never engages.
  [INF] J2  200 mm closest-viewing distance (Sec 11) is not applied: all receptors are >= 10 m from the panel (site geometry), and the default IEC/SGHAT observer is a point at its georeferenced position, not at the 0.2 m near-point.
  [INF] J3  ICNIRP integration ranges are printed inconsistently: in-body Eqs 4/10/11 print 380-1400 nm for both L_R and L_B; the errata page 87 line corrects Eq 10/11 to 300-700 nm for the blue-light hazard (B(λ)=0.01 below 380 makes the difference negligible); Eq 4 (thermal) legitimately stays 380-1400 nm because ICNIRP's R(lambda) tails cover 1400. The pipeline integrates the full 200-4000 nm spectrum once for k_X, so it is insensitive to this editorial inconsistency.
  [INF] J4  ICNIRP is an ELV guideline (limits only), NOT a risk-group taxonomy; the RG1/RG2/RG3 classification is IEC 62471 Table 6.1's. The mapping used here -- RG3/IEC == exceeds the ICNIRP steady retina ELV (2.8e4/alpha) -- is our documented bridge between the two references and is exactly what section [I] asserts.
  [INF] J5  dose vs average-power: ICNIRP permits checking either the radiance dose D_R or the average radiance L_R; the pipeline uses the radiance form (L_R) with the t_max inversion, which is the more readable safety statement for a continuous glare minute (Eq 4.9/6 inverse). The dose cross-check is redundant given the constant-radiance assumption.
  [INF] J6  89-degree grazing clip, DNI floor and the sun-disc front-face guard are SGHAT/pipeline additions, orthogonal to the standard limits and already documented -- this script only validates the exposure-limit reference chain.

Summary: 92 passed, 2 failed, 22 info

[J] INFO: documented simplifications / conventions
======================================================================
  [INF] J1  alpha_min divergence ICNIRP 0.0015 rad vs IEC 0.0017 rad is benign on the current config: every hazard alpha sits at ~0.05 rad (sun-disc-limited far field), far above both flooring values, so the clamp never engages.
  [INF] J2  200 mm closest-viewing distance (Sec 11) is not applied: all receptors are >= 10 m from the panel (site geometry), and the default IEC/SGHAT observer is a point at its georeferenced position, not at the 0.2 m near-point.
  [INF] J3  ICNIRP integration ranges are printed inconsistently: in-body Eqs 4/10/11 print 380-1400 nm for both L_R and L_B; the errata page 87 line corrects Eq 10/11 to 300-700 nm for the blue-light hazard (B(λ)=0.01 below 380 makes the difference negligible); Eq 4 (thermal) legitimately stays 380-1400 nm because ICNIRP's R(lambda) tails cover 1400. The pipeline integrates the full 200-4000 nm spectrum once for k_X, so it is insensitive to this editorial inconsistency.
  [INF] J4  ICNIRP is an ELV guideline (limits only), NOT a risk-group taxonomy; the RG1/RG2/RG3 classification is IEC 62471 Table 6.1's. The mapping used here -- RG3/IEC == exceeds the ICNIRP steady retina ELV (2.8e4/alpha) -- is our documented bridge between the two references and is exactly what section [I] asserts.
  [INF] J5  dose vs average-power: ICNIRP permits checking either the radiance dose D_R or the average radiance L_R; the pipeline uses the radiance form (L_R) with the t_max inversion, which is the more readable safety statement for a continuous glare minute (Eq 4.9/6 inverse). The dose cross-check is redundant given the constant-radiance assumption.
  [INF] J6  89-degree grazing clip, DNI floor and the sun-disc front-face guard are SGHAT/pipeline additions, orthogonal to the standard limits and already documented -- this script only validates the exposure-limit reference chain.

======================================================================
[J] INFO: documented simplifications / conventions
======================================================================
  [INF] J1  alpha_min divergence ICNIRP 0.0015 rad vs IEC 0.0017 rad is benign on the current config: every hazard alpha sits at ~0.05 rad (sun-disc-limited far field), far above both flooring values, so the clamp never engages.
  [INF] J2  200 mm closest-viewing distance (Sec 11) is not applied: all receptors are >= 10 m from the panel (site geometry), and the default IEC/SGHAT observer is a point at its georeferenced position, not at the 0.2 m near-point.
  [INF] J3  ICNIRP integration ranges are printed inconsistently: in-body Eqs 4/10/11 print 380-1400 nm for both L_R and L_B; the errata page 87 line corrects Eq 10/11 to 300-700 nm for the blue-light hazard (B(λ)=0.01 below 380 makes the difference negligible); Eq 4 (thermal) legitimately stays 380-1400 nm because ICNIRP's R(lambda) tails cover 1400. The pipeline integrates the full 200-4000 nm spectrum once for k_X, so it is insensitive to this editorial inconsistency.
  [INF] J4  ICNIRP is an ELV guideline (limits only), NOT a risk-group taxonomy; the RG1/RG2/RG3 classification is IEC 62471 Table 6.1's. The mapping used here -- RG3/IEC == exceeds the ICNIRP steady retina ELV (2.8e4/alpha) -- is our documented bridge between the two references and is exactly what section [I] asserts.
  [INF] J5  dose vs average-power: ICNIRP permits checking either the radiance dose D_R or the average radiance L_R; the pipeline uses the radiance form (L_R) with the t_max inversion, which is the more readable safety statement for a continuous glare minute (Eq 4.9/6 inverse). The dose cross-check is redundant given the constant-radiance assumption.
  [INF] J6  89-degree grazing clip, DNI floor and the sun-disc front-face guard are SGHAT/pipeline additions, orthogonal to the standard limits and already documented -- this script only validates the exposure-limit reference chain.

[J] INFO: documented simplifications / conventions
======================================================================
  [INF] J1  alpha_min divergence ICNIRP 0.0015 rad vs IEC 0.0017 rad is benign on the current config: every hazard alpha sits at ~0.05 rad (sun-disc-limited far field), far above both flooring values, so the clamp never engages.
  [INF] J2  200 mm closest-viewing distance (Sec 11) is not applied: all receptors are >= 10 m from the panel (site geometry), and the default IEC/SGHAT observer is a point at its georeferenced position, not at the 0.2 m near-point.
  [INF] J3  ICNIRP integration ranges are printed inconsistently: in-body Eqs 4/10/11 print 380-1400 nm for both L_R and L_B; the errata page 87 line corrects Eq 10/11 to 300-700 nm for the blue-light hazard (B(λ)=0.01 below 380 makes the difference negligible); Eq 4 (thermal) legitimately stays 380-1400 nm because ICNIRP's R(lambda) tails cover 1400. The pipeline integrates the full 200-4000 nm spectrum once for k_X, so it is insensitive to this editorial inconsistency.
  [INF] J4  ICNIRP is an ELV guideline (limits only), NOT a risk-group taxonomy; the RG1/RG2/RG3 classification is IEC 62471 Table 6.1's. The mapping used here -- RG3/IEC == exceeds the ICNIRP steady retina ELV (2.8e4/alpha) -- is our documented bridge between the two references and is exactly what section [I] asserts.
  [INF] J5  dose vs average-power: ICNIRP permits checking either the radiance dose D_R or the average radiance L_R; the pipeline uses the radiance form (L_R) with the t_max inversion, which is the more readable safety statement for a continuous glare minute (Eq 4.9/6 inverse). The dose cross-check is redundant given the constant-radiance assumption.
  [INF] J6  89-degree grazing clip, DNI floor and the sun-disc front-face guard are SGHAT/pipeline additions, orthogonal to the standard limits and already documented -- this script only validates the exposure-limit reference chain.

[J] INFO: documented simplifications / conventions
======================================================================
  [INF] J1  alpha_min divergence ICNIRP 0.0015 rad vs IEC 0.0017 rad is benign on the current config: every hazard alpha sits at ~0.05 rad (sun-disc-limited far field), far above both flooring values, so the clamp never engages.
  [INF] J2  200 mm closest-viewing distance (Sec 11) is not applied: all receptors are >= 10 m from the panel (site geometry), and the default IEC/SGHAT observer is a point at its georeferenced position, not at the 0.2 m near-point.
  [INF] J3  ICNIRP integration ranges are printed inconsistently: in-body Eqs 4/10/11 print 380-1400 nm for both L_R and L_B; the errata page 87 line corrects Eq 10/11 to 300-700 nm for the blue-light hazard (B(λ)=0.01 below 380 makes the difference negligible); Eq 4 (thermal) legitimately stays 380-1400 nm because ICNIRP's R(lambda) tails cover 1400. The pipeline integrates the full 200-4000 nm spectrum once for k_X, so it is insensitive to this editorial inconsistency.
  [INF] J4  ICNIRP is an ELV guideline (limits only), NOT a risk-group taxonomy; the RG1/RG2/RG3 classification is IEC 62471 Table 6.1's. The mapping used here -- RG3/IEC == exceeds the ICNIRP steady retina ELV (2.8e4/alpha) -- is our documented bridge between the two references and is exactly what section [I] asserts.
  [INF] J5  dose vs average-power: ICNIRP permits checking either the radiance dose D_R or the average radiance L_R; the pipeline uses the radiance form (L_R) with the t_max inversion, which is the more readable safety statement for a continuous glare minute (Eq 4.9/6 inverse). The dose cross-check is redundant given the constant-radiance assumption.
  [INF] J6  89-degree grazing clip, DNI floor and the sun-disc front-face guard are SGHAT/pipeline additions, orthogonal to the standard limits and already documented -- this script only validates the exposure-limit reference chain.
